Palmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_M._Palmer_\(politician\)), the [National Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Democratic_Party_\(United_States\)) nominee, its only endorsement for a candidate who is not a member of the Republican Party or the Democratic Party.<sup>[\[189\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdamsLouttitTaylor2016-198)</sup>

[^cite_note-291]: &#x20;The national edition of *The New York Times* uses 11.5 inches (290 mm) pages.<sup>[\[280\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDunlap2016f-290)</sup>

[^cite_note-363]: &#x20;Attributed to multiple references:<sup>[\[349\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMartinHansen19987-360)</sup><sup>[\[350\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchwarz201229-361)</sup><sup>[\[351\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTESterling20091020-362)</sup>

[^cite_note-378]: &#x20;A 2003 study in the *[Harvard International Journal of Press/Politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_International_Journal_of_Press/Politics)* concluded that *The New York Times* reporting was more favorable to Israelis than to Palestinians.<sup>[\[361\]](#cite_note-viser-373)</sup> A 2002 study published in the journal *[Journalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Journalism_\(journal\))* examined Middle East coverage of the [Second Intifada](71717.md) over a one-month period in *The New York Times*, *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* and the *[Chicago Tribune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Tribune)*. The study authors said that the *Times* was "the most slanted in a pro-Israeli direction" with a bias "reflected...in its use of headlines, photographs, graphics, sourcing practices, and lead paragraphs."<sup>[\[362\]](#cite_note-374)</sup> A *[Media, War & Conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media,_War_&_Conflict)* study based on a quantitative analysis of use of [active](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Active_voice) and [passive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passive_voice) voice and of the sentiment of the language used during the [first](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Intifada) and [second](71717.md) Palestinian intifadas found the paper's coverage of the events was disproportionately anti-Palestinian and that such bias worsened from the First Intifada to the Second.<sup>[\[363\]](#cite_note-375)</sup> \
    &#x20;A 2024 piece in the journal *[Israel Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Affairs?action=edit\&redlink=1)* also found "errors, omissions, and poor editorial supervision" in the paper's coverage of the Gaza war, arguing: The casualty reports throughout the war relied almost solely on the Gaza Ministry of Health, which is controlled by Hamas, and frequently produced false and inflated data. Also, the repeated omissions of IDF statements about journalists that were active Hamas operatives showed that the Times preferred to believe Aljazeera, a questionable news organisation serving an autocratic leader, over the credibility of the IDF, a formal institution of a democratic state.<sup>[\[364\]](#cite_note-376)</sup> [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and others have also accused *The New York Times* of bias against Israel, accusations that [Eric Alterman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Alterman) has deemed false.<sup>[\[365\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlterman2023-377)</sup>

[^cite_note-416]: &#x20;Attributed to multiple references:<sup>[\[399\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMigdon2023-412)</sup><sup>[\[400\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEYurcaba2023-413)</sup><sup>[\[401\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKalish2023-414)</sup><sup>[\[402\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEYang2023-415)</sup>

[^cite_note-viser-373]: &#x20;Viser, Matt (September 2003). "Attempted Objectivity: An Analysis of the New York Times and Ha'aretz and their Portrayals of the Palestinian-Israeli Conflict". *The International Journal of Press/Politics*. **8** (4): 114–120. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1177/1081180X03256999](https://doi.org/10.1177%2F1081180X03256999). [S2CID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S2CID_\(identifier\)) [145209853](https://api.semanticscholar.org/CorpusID\:145209853). This study explores the biases, pro-Israeli and pro-Palestinian, by looking at quantitative indicators of news coverage in *The New York Times* and *Ha'aretz*. Several time periods were examined (1987–88, 2000–01, and post-September 11, 2001), using multiple indicators. By these measures, *The New York Times* is more favorable toward the Israelis than the Palestinians, and the partiality has become more pronounced with time.
